# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title ipo Model

class Application {
    amountOfMoney : double
    share : int
    status : ApplicationStatus
    allowance : Document
    company : Company
    customer : Customer
    emails : List<Email>
    approve() : boolean
    cancel() : boolean
    reject() : boolean
    sendRejectionEmail() : void
}
class Company {
    email : String
    name : String
}
class Customer {
    canApplyForIPO : boolean
    email : String
    name : String
    surname : String
    telephone : String
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@519c6fcc (name: createApplication) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Creates a new IPO application for the customer. The operation checks that the customer
is eligible to apply (canApplyForIPO is true and isEligibleForIPO returns true), that there is no
existing approved application for the same company, and that input parameters are valid
(shares > 0, amount > 0, doc is not null). If all checks pass, a new Application is created
with PENDING status and added to the customer's applications list. Returns true on success.
Algorithm:
  if canApplyForIPO == false or isEligibleForIPO() == false then return false
  if shares <= 0 or amount <= 0 or doc == null then return false
  for each app in applications:
      if app.company.name == company.name and app.status == APPROVAL then return false
  Application newApp = new Application()
  newApp.amountOfMoney = amount
  newApp.share = shares
  newApp.status = PENDING
  newApp.allowance = doc
  newApp.company = company
  newApp.customer = this
  newApp.emails = new ArrayList<>()
  applications.add(newApp)
  return true
Related Features: canApplyForIPO, isEligibleForIPO(), applications list, Application constructor,
  Application::status, Company::name
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input:
  company : Company - the target company for the IPO application
  shares : int - number of shares requested (must be > 0)
  amount : double - payment amount (must be > 0)
  doc : Document - legal allowance documentation (must be non-null)
Output: boolean - true if the application was created successfully, false otherwise
Pre-condition: The customer object exists. The company object exists and has a valid name.
Post-condition: If return is true, a new Application is added to the customer's applications list with PENDING status.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

```plantuml
    REJECTED

@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Application::sendRejectionEmail
Summary: Creates and sends a rejection email to the customer. The email content includes the customer's
name, surname, email address, telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares,
and the paid amount. This method is called internally by the reject() operation.
Algorithm:
  Email rejectionEmail = new Email()
  rejectionEmail.receiver = customer.email
  rejectionEmail.content = createEmailContent(company, share, customer, amountOfMoney)
  emails.add(rejectionEmail)
Related Features: customer, company, share, amountOfMoney, emails, Email::createEmailContent
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email
Input: None (uses instance fields)
Output: void
Pre-condition: The application has valid customer, company, share, and amountOfMoney fields.
Post-condition: A new Email object is created with the rejection content and added to the emails list.

Application::reject
Summary: Rejects a pending IPO application. The operation updates the application status to REJECTED,
sends a rejection email to the customer, and returns true on success.
Rejection is only allowed if the application is currently pending.
Algorithm:
  if status != PENDING then return false
  status = REJECTED
  sendRejectionEmail()
  return true
Related Features: status, sendRejectionEmail()
Related Classes: ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if rejection succeeded, false if application is not pending
Pre-condition: The application exists and is in PENDING status.
Post-condition: If return is true, status is set to REJECTED and a rejection email is sent to the customer.
If return is false, the application state remains unchanged.

Application::cancel
Summary: Cancels a pending IPO application. Only applications with PENDING status can be canceled.
The operation updates the application status to a canceled state (not explicitly defined, assumed as a new status or removal).
According to the class diagram, there is no CANCELED status in the enum, so we infer a conceptual cancellation.
For practical purposes, we interpret that the application is removed from the customer's list or marked as canceled.
Since the enum only has PENDING, APPROVAL, REJECTED, we assume the application is removed from the system.
Algorithm:
  if status != PENDING then return false
  // Remove this application from customer's applications list
  customer.applications.remove(this)
  return true
Related Features: status, customer.applications
Related Classes: Customer, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if cancellation succeeded, false if application is not pending
Pre-condition: The application exists and is in PENDING status.
Post-condition: If return is true, the application is removed from the customer's applications list.
If return is false, the application state remains unchanged.

Application::approve
Summary: Approves a pending IPO application. The operation updates the application status to APPROVAL,
sends two information emails (one to the customer and one to the company), and returns true on success.
Approval is only allowed if the application is currently pending and the related customer is still eligible for IPO.
Algorithm:
  if status != PENDING then return false
  if customer.isEligibleForIPO() == false then return false
  status = APPROVAL
  // Create and send email to customer
  Email customerEmail = new Email()
  customerEmail.receiver = customer.email
  customerEmail.content = "Your IPO application for " + company.name + " has been approved."
  emails.add(customerEmail)
  // Create and send email to company
  Email companyEmail = new Email()
  companyEmail.receiver = company.email
  companyEmail.content = "Customer " + customer.name + " " + customer.surname + " has been approved for IPO in your company."
  emails.add(companyEmail)
  return true
Related Features: status, customer, company, emails (list), customer.isEligibleForIPO()
Related Classes: Customer, Company, Email, ApplicationStatus
Input: None (operates on current Application instance)
Output: boolean - true if approval succeeded, false if application is not pending or customer is ineligible
Pre-condition: The application exists and is in PENDING status. The customer object is not null.
Post-condition: If return is true, status is set to APPROVAL, and two emails are added to the emails list.
If return is false, the application state remains unchanged.

Post-condition: If return is true, the application is removed from the customer's applications list.

# Requirement
```text
Retail customers should be eligible for applying IPO. Customers who will make IPO Applications must share their name, surname, email address, and telephone number information with the related company.

When a customer applies IPO, he is required to upload legal allowance documentation. Each document has a name that identifies the allowance document. When the customer completes the application process successfully, the bank should send two information emails, one for the customer and another for the company. If the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer from the application process. If they would like to apply anyway, they should call the "call center" and request IPO application access.

Even though the customer completed the process successfully, the purchase is still being determined. Related companies always have a right to reject customers' applications. If a company rejects the customer, our system should inform the customer. Email content should include the customer's name, surname, email address, and telephone number, the applied company name, the number of shares that have been purchased, and the amount of money that has been paid.

Functional requirement:
1. Create an IPO application. A retail customer starts an IPO application by providing the target company, the number of shares greater than 0, the payment amount greater than 0, and a non-null allowance document. The system checks that the customer is currently eligible to apply and has no approved application for the same company name. The operation returns true when the application record is created successfully; otherwise, false.
2. Approve or reject an application. The bank can approve or reject only a pending application. Approval is allowed only when the related customer is still eligible to apply for IPO. When the bank approves an application, the system sends two information emails, one to the customer and one to the company. When the bank rejects an application, the system sends one rejection email to the customer, and the rejection email content includes the customer's name, surname, email address, telephone number, the company name, the number of shares, and the paid amount. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.
3. Retrieve a customer's application-count summary. The customer can view how many IPO applications have been completed in total. Only approved and rejected applications are counted. Pending applications and canceled applications are not counted. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
4. Query the total approved IPO application amount for a customer. The customer can view the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications. Pending and rejected applications are excluded from the total. The operation returns 0 when the customer has no approved applications.
5. Cancel a pending application. By providing a company name, a customer can cancel an application that is still pending. Approved and rejected applications cannot be canceled. The operation returns true when cancellation succeeds; otherwise, false.
```

Email::createEmailContent

# Operation Specifications
Customer::createApplication
Summary: Creates a new IPO application for the customer. The operation checks that the customer
is eligible to apply (canApplyForIPO is true and isEligibleForIPO returns true), that there is no
existing approved application for the same company, and that input parameters are valid
(shares > 0, amount > 0, doc is not null). If all checks pass, a new Application is created
with PENDING status and added to the customer's applications list. Returns true on success.
Algorithm:
  if canApplyForIPO == false or isEligibleForIPO() == false then return false
  if shares <= 0 or amount <= 0 or doc == null then return false
  for each app in applications:
      if app.company.name == company.name and app.status == APPROVAL then return false
  Application newApp = new Application()
  newApp.amountOfMoney = amount
  newApp.share = shares
  newApp.status = PENDING
  newApp.allowance = doc
  newApp.company = company
  newApp.customer = this
  newApp.emails = new ArrayList<>()
  applications.add(newApp)
  return true
Related Features: canApplyForIPO, isEligibleForIPO(), applications list, Application constructor,
  Application::status, Company::name
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input:
  company : Company - the target company for the IPO application
  shares : int - number of shares requested (must be > 0)
  amount : double - payment amount (must be > 0)
  doc : Document - legal allowance documentation (must be non-null)
Output: boolean - true if the application was created successfully, false otherwise
Pre-condition: The customer object exists. The company object exists and has a valid name.
Post-condition: If return is true, a new Application is added to the customer's applications list with PENDING status.
If return is false, no changes are made to the system.

Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Checks whether the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO.
The eligibility is based on the canApplyForIPO flag and potentially other business rules
(e.g., not having too many failed attempts). For this implementation, it simply returns the canApplyForIPO flag.
According to the requirement, if the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer,
which would set canApplyForIPO to false.
Algorithm:
  return canApplyForIPO
Related Features: canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if the customer is eligible to apply for IPO, false otherwise
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: No changes to the system state.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Returns the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications for the customer.
Pending, rejected, and canceled applications are excluded. Returns 0.0 if the customer has no approved applications.
Algorithm:
  total = 0.0
  for each app in applications:
      if app.status == APPROVAL:
          total = total + app.amountOfMoney
  return total
Related Features: applications list, Application::status, Application::amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: double - the total sum of paid amounts for approved applications (non-negative)
Pre-condition: The customer's applications list may be empty.
Post-condition: No changes to the system state.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Returns the count of IPO applications that have been completed (approved or rejected).
Pending and canceled applications are not counted. Returns 0 if the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
Algorithm:
  count = 0
  for each app in applications:
      if app.status == APPROVAL or app.status == REJECTED:
          count = count + 1
  return count
Related Features: applications list, Application::status
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int - the number of approved or rejected applications (non-negative integer)
Pre-condition: The customer's applications list may be empty.
Post-condition: No changes to the system state.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Allows a customer to cancel a pending IPO application by providing the company name.
The operation finds the application with the given company name (that is in PENDING status)
and calls its cancel() method. Returns true if cancellation succeeds, otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  for each app in applications:
      if app.company.name == companyName and app.status == PENDING:
          return app.cancel()
  return false
Related Features: applications list, Application::cancel, Application::status, Company::name
Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
Input: companyName : String - the name of the company for which the application was made
Output: boolean - true if the pending application was found and canceled, false otherwise
Pre-condition: The customer must have at least one application (list may be empty).
Post-condition: If a matching pending application is found, it is removed from the applications list.
Otherwise, the applications list remains unchanged.

```plantuml
    applications : List<Application>
    cancelApplication(companyName : String) : boolean
    createApplication(company : Company, shares : int, amount : double, doc : Document) : boolean
    getApplicationCount() : int
    getApprovedTotalAmount() : double
    isEligibleForIPO() : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Email {
    content : String
    receiver : String
    createEmailContent(company : Company, shares : int, customer : Customer, amount : double) : String
}
enum ApplicationStatus {
    PENDING
    APPROVAL
    REJECTED
}

Application --> Document : allowance
Application --> Company : company
Application --> Customer : customer  'opposite: applications
Application *-- "*" Email : emails
Customer *-- "*" Application : applications  'opposite: customer
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
Customer::createApplication
Summary: Creates a new IPO application for the customer. The operation checks that the customer
is eligible to apply (canApplyForIPO is true and isEligibleForIPO returns true), that there is no
existing approved application for the same company, and that input parameters are valid
(shares > 0, amount > 0, doc is not null). If all checks pass, a new Application is created
with PENDING status and added to the customer's applications list. Returns true on success.
Algorithm:
  if canApplyForIPO == false or isEligibleForIPO() == false then return false
  if shares <= 0 or amount <= 0 or doc == null then return false
  for each app in applications:
      if app.company.name == company.name and app.status == APPROVAL then return false
  Application newApp = new Application()
  newApp.amountOfMoney = amount
  newApp.share = shares
  newApp.status = PENDING
  newApp.allowance = doc
  newApp.company = company
  newApp.customer = this
  newApp.emails = new ArrayList<>()
  applications.add(newApp)
  return true
Related Features: canApplyForIPO, isEligibleForIPO(), applications list, Application constructor,
  Application::status, Company::name
Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
Input:
  company : Company - the target company for the IPO application
  shares : int - number of shares requested (must be > 0)
  amount : double - payment amount (must be > 0)
  doc : Document - legal allowance documentation (must be non-null)
Output: boolean - true if the application was created successfully, false otherwise
Pre-condition: The customer object exists. The company object exists and has a valid name.
Post-condition: If return is true, a new Application is added to the customer's applications list with PENDING status.
If return is false, no changes are made to the system.

Customer::isEligibleForIPO
Summary: Checks whether the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO.
The eligibility is based on the canApplyForIPO flag and potentially other business rules
(e.g., not having too many failed attempts). For this implementation, it simply returns the canApplyForIPO flag.
According to the requirement, if the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer,
which would set canApplyForIPO to false.
Algorithm:
  return canApplyForIPO
Related Features: canApplyForIPO
Related Classes: None
Input: None
Output: boolean - true if the customer is eligible to apply for IPO, false otherwise
Pre-condition: None
Post-condition: No changes to the system state.

Customer::getApprovedTotalAmount
Summary: Returns the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications for the customer.
Pending, rejected, and canceled applications are excluded. Returns 0.0 if the customer has no approved applications.
Algorithm:
  total = 0.0
  for each app in applications:
      if app.status == APPROVAL:
          total = total + app.amountOfMoney
  return total
Related Features: applications list, Application::status, Application::amountOfMoney
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: double - the total sum of paid amounts for approved applications (non-negative)
Pre-condition: The customer's applications list may be empty.
Post-condition: No changes to the system state.

Customer::getApplicationCount
Summary: Returns the count of IPO applications that have been completed (approved or rejected).
Pending and canceled applications are not counted. Returns 0 if the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
Algorithm:
  count = 0
  for each app in applications:
      if app.status == APPROVAL or app.status == REJECTED:
          count = count + 1
  return count
Related Features: applications list, Application::status
Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
Input: None
Output: int - the number of approved or rejected applications (non-negative integer)
Pre-condition: The customer's applications list may be empty.
Post-condition: No changes to the system state.

Customer::cancelApplication
Summary: Allows a customer to cancel a pending IPO application by providing the company name.
The operation finds the application with the given company name (that is in PENDING status)
and calls its cancel() method. Returns true if cancellation succeeds, otherwise false.
Algorithm:
  for each app in applications:
      if app.company.name == companyName and app.status == PENDING:
          return app.cancel()
  return false
Related Features: applications list, Application::cancel, Application::status, Company::name
Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
Input: companyName : String - the name of the company for which the application was made
Output: boolean - true if the pending application was found and canceled, false otherwise
Pre-condition: The customer must have at least one application (list may be empty).
Post-condition: If a matching pending application is found, it is removed from the applications list.
Otherwise, the applications list remains unchanged.

    content += "Company: " + company.getName() + "\n";
    content += "Shares: " + shares + "\n";
    content += "Amount Paid: " + amount + "\n";
    return content;

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
   * with PENDING status and added to the customer's applications list. Returns true on success.

   *   if canApplyForIPO == false or isEligibleForIPO() == false then return false
   *   if shares <= 0 or amount <= 0 or doc == null then return false
   *   for each app in applications:
   *       if app.company.name == company.name and app.status == APPROVAL then return false
   *   Application newApp = new Application()
   *   newApp.amountOfMoney = amount
   *   newApp.share = shares
   *   newApp.status = PENDING

  public boolean createApplication(final Company company, final int shares, final double amount, final Document doc)
  {
```

```java
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (shares <= 0 || amount <= 0 || doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    for (Application app : getApplications())
    {
      if (app.getCompany().getName().equals(company.getName()) && app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL)
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    Application newApp = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createApplication();
    newApp.setAmountOfMoney(amount);
    newApp.setShare(shares);
    newApp.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.PENDING);
    newApp.setAllowance(doc);
    newApp.setCompany(company);
    newApp.setCustomer(this);
    newApp.getEmails().clear();
    getApplications().add(newApp);
    return true;
```

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Creates a new IPO application for the customer. The operation checks that the customer
   * is eligible to apply (canApplyForIPO is true and isEligibleForIPO returns true), that there is no
   * existing approved application for the same company, and that input parameters are valid
   * (shares > 0, amount > 0, doc is not null). If all checks pass, a new Application is created
   * with PENDING status and added to the customer's applications list. Returns true on success.
   * Algorithm:
   *   if canApplyForIPO == false or isEligibleForIPO() == false then return false
   *   if shares <= 0 or amount <= 0 or doc == null then return false
   *   for each app in applications:
   *       if app.company.name == company.name and app.status == APPROVAL then return false
   *   Application newApp = new Application()
   *   newApp.amountOfMoney = amount
   *   newApp.share = shares
   *   newApp.status = PENDING
   *   newApp.allowance = doc
   *   newApp.company = company
   *   newApp.customer = this
   *   newApp.emails = new ArrayList<>()
   *   applications.add(newApp)
   *   return true
   * Related Features: canApplyForIPO, isEligibleForIPO(), applications list, Application constructor,
   *   Application::status, Company::name
   * Related Classes: Application, Company, Document, ApplicationStatus
   * Input:
   *   company : Company - the target company for the IPO application
   *   shares : int - number of shares requested (must be > 0)
   *   amount : double - payment amount (must be > 0)
   *   doc : Document - legal allowance documentation (must be non-null)
   * Output: boolean - true if the application was created successfully, false otherwise
   * Pre-condition: The customer object exists. The company object exists and has a valid name.
   * Post-condition: If return is true, a new Application is added to the customer's applications list with PENDING status.
   * If return is false, no changes are made to the system.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean createApplication(final Company company, final int shares, final double amount, final Document doc)
  {
    if (isCanApplyForIPO() == false || isEligibleForIPO() == false)
    {
      return false;
    }
    if (shares <= 0 || amount <= 0 || doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    for (Application app : getApplications())
    {
      if (app.getCompany().getName().equals(company.getName()) && app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL)
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    Application newApp = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createApplication();
    newApp.setAmountOfMoney(amount);
    newApp.setShare(shares);
    newApp.setStatus(ApplicationStatus.PENDING);
    newApp.setAllowance(doc);
    newApp.setCompany(company);
    newApp.setCustomer(this);
    newApp.getEmails().clear();
    getApplications().add(newApp);
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Returns the count of IPO applications that have been completed (approved or rejected).
   * Pending and canceled applications are not counted. Returns 0 if the customer has no approved or rejected applications.
   * Algorithm:
   *   count = 0
   *   for each app in applications:
   *       if app.status == APPROVAL or app.status == REJECTED:
   *           count = count + 1
   *   return count
   * Related Features: applications list, Application::status
   * Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
   * Input: None
   * Output: int - the number of approved or rejected applications (non-negative integer)
   * Pre-condition: The customer's applications list may be empty.
   * Post-condition: No changes to the system state.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model kind="operation"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public int getApplicationCount()
  {
    int count = 0;
    for (Application app : getApplications())
    {
      if (app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL || app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.REJECTED)
      {
        count = count + 1;
      }
    }
    return count;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Returns the sum of the paid amounts of all approved IPO applications for the customer.
   * Pending, rejected, and canceled applications are excluded. Returns 0.0 if the customer has no approved applications.
   * Algorithm:
   *   total = 0.0
   *   for each app in applications:
   *       if app.status == APPROVAL:
   *           total = total + app.amountOfMoney
   *   return total
   * Related Features: applications list, Application::status, Application::amountOfMoney
   * Related Classes: Application, ApplicationStatus
   * Input: None
   * Output: double - the total sum of paid amounts for approved applications (non-negative)
   * Pre-condition: The customer's applications list may be empty.
   * Post-condition: No changes to the system state.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model kind="operation"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double getApprovedTotalAmount()
  {
    double total = 0.0;
    for (Application app : getApplications())
    {
      if (app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL)
      {
        total = total + app.getAmountOfMoney();
      }
    }
    return total;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Allows a customer to cancel a pending IPO application by providing the company name.
   * The operation finds the application with the given company name (that is in PENDING status)
   * and calls its cancel() method. Returns true if cancellation succeeds, otherwise false.
   * Algorithm:
   *   for each app in applications:
   *       if app.company.name == companyName and app.status == PENDING:
   *           return app.cancel()
   *   return false
   * Related Features: applications list, Application::cancel, Application::status, Company::name
   * Related Classes: Application, Company, ApplicationStatus
   * Input: companyName : String - the name of the company for which the application was made
   * Output: boolean - true if the pending application was found and canceled, false otherwise
   * Pre-condition: None
   * Post-condition: If a matching pending application is found, it is canceled (status changed to CANCELED or removed from the pending set).
   * Otherwise, the applications list remains unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean cancelApplication(final String companyName)
  {
    for (Application app : getApplications())
    {
      if (app.getCompany().getName().equals(companyName) && app.getStatus() == ApplicationStatus.PENDING)
      {
        return app.cancel();
      }
    }
    return false;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Checks whether the customer is currently eligible to apply for IPO.
   * The eligibility is based on the canApplyForIPO flag and potentially other business rules
   * (e.g., not having too many failed attempts). For this implementation, it simply returns the canApplyForIPO flag.
   * According to the requirement, if the customer has many failed attempts, the bank should restrict the customer,
   * which would set canApplyForIPO to false.
   * Algorithm:
   *   return canApplyForIPO
   * Related Features: canApplyForIPO

   * @model kind="operation"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT

    return isCanApplyForIPO();

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Navigate the Ecore model to find related classes
  1.2 Also consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.3 Locate these files in your project.
  1.4 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the **Context**.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
    {
```
      return false;
    }
    if (getCustomer().isEligibleForIPO() == false)
    {
      return false;
    }
    setStatus(ApplicationStatus.APPROVAL);
    // Create and send email to customer
    Email customerEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    customerEmail.setReceiver(getCustomer().getEmail());
    customerEmail.setContent("Your IPO application for " + getCompany().getName() + " has been approved.");
    getEmails().add(customerEmail);
    // Create and send email to company
    Email companyEmail = IpoFactory.eINSTANCE.createEmail();
    companyEmail.setReceiver(getCompany().getEmail());
    companyEmail.setContent("Customer " + getCustomer().getName() + " " + getCustomer().getSurname() + " has been approved for IPO in your company.");
    getEmails().add(companyEmail);
    return true;

    getEmails().add(rejectionEmail);
  }
